. Applying this principle a second time implies that the following is true. Suppose that
x_{-}^{\prime\prime}\in(x_{-},b_{-})_{\partial\mathbf{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
and
x_{+}^{\prime\prime}\in(x_{+},b_{+})_{\partial\mathbf{D}}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
. Then on
E
it is a positive conditional probability event given
\Upsilon
that the loops of
\Gamma_{V}^{\prime}
which hit either
[x_{-}^{\prime},x_{+}^{\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[x_{-}^{\prime\prime},x_{+}^{\prime\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
do not disconnect
x_{-}
from
x_{+}
in
V
. Let
W
be the complementary component in
V
of these loops which has
x_{-},x_{+}
on its boundary. We note that the loops
\Gamma_{W}^{\prime}
of
\Gamma_{V}^{\prime}
which are contained in
\mathrm{cl}(W)
have the law of a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
in
W
.

On the above event, we note that there a.s. exists a loop
\mathcal{L}_{-}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{+}^{\prime}
) of
\Gamma_{W}^{\prime}
which disconnects
x_{-}
(resp.
x_{+}
) from
x_{+}
(resp.
x_{-}
) and does not hit either
[x_{-}^{\prime},x_{+}^{\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[x_{-}^{\prime\prime},x_{+}^{\prime\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
. Since the graph of
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
loops is a.s. connected, there a.s. exists
n
loops
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\prime}
in
\Gamma_{W}^{\prime}
so that
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{-}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
,
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{+}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, and
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{j-1}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
for each
2\leq j\leq n
. Thus applying the resampling procedure one more time, it is a positive probability event that
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\prime}
get connected into a single
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
loop in
\mathrm{cl}(W)
which disconnects
x_{-}
from
x_{+}
and vice-versa. On this event, the branch of the exploration tree of the resulting
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}
does not hit either
[x_{-}^{\prime},x_{+}^{\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowright}}
or
[x_{-}^{\prime\prime},x_{+}^{\prime\prime}]_{\partial V}^{\bm{\circlearrowleft}}
and is a CPI of
\Gamma_{V}
. Altogether, this completes the proof.
∎

3.3.
Crossing a disk bounded by CPIs away from the boundary

Figure 3.2.
Illustration of the statement of Lemma
3.4
. The light blue curve is the trunk of
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
and the green curve is the trunk of
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
. The left boundary (
\eta_{0}
and its time-reversal
\overline{\eta}_{0}
) of
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
(as viewed from
\eta_{+}(Y_{0})
) is shown in dark blue and the left boundary (
\eta_{1}
) of
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
(as viewed from
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
) is shown in dark green. The
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
loops visited by
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
are not shown as we have taken them to be
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
processes (i.e.,
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
loops are only on the left side of the trunk) so that
\partial\mathcal{D}
is only composed of parts of the boundaries of the trunks of
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
and not of
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
loops.

Lemma 3.4
.

For each
\zeta_{0},p\in(0,1)
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
so that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following is true. Let
X_{0}
be an exponential random variable with mean
\delta
and let
Y_{0}
be uniform in
[0,X_{0}]
, independently of everything else. Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
\eta_{+}(X_{0})
to
\eta_{+}(Y_{0})
coupled with
\Gamma_{+}
as a CPI, let
\eta_{0}
be its left boundary, assume that
\eta_{0}
is parameterized according to quantum length, and let
L
be the total quantum length of
\eta_{0}
. Given
L
, let
X_{1},Y_{1}
be i.i.d. uniform in
[0,L]
independently of everything else. Let
\overline{\eta}_{0}
be the time-reversal of
\eta_{0}
. On the event that
\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cap\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}])=\emptyset
and the part of
\eta_{0}
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
(i.e.,
\eta_{0}|_{[X_{1},L-Y_{1}]}
) does not hit
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
, let
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
process in the unbounded component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus\eta_{0}
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
, coupled as a CPI of the loops of
\Gamma_{+}
in this component. Let
\eta_{1}
be the left boundary of
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
. Let
Z_{1}
be uniform in
[X_{1},L-Y_{1}]
and let
\mathcal{D}
be the component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus(\eta_{0}\cup\eta_{1})
with
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
on its boundary. Let
E
be the event that there does not exist a path
\omega
in
\mathcal{D}\cap\Upsilon_{+}
from
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
to
\eta_{1}
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Then
\mathbf{P}[E]\leq\zeta_{0}
.

The strategy to prove Lemma
3.4
is the following. First, we will prove a technical result (Lemma
3.5
) which gives that the conditional law of the intermediate part of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{1};\rho_{2})
process is absolutely continuous with respect to ordinary
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
on the event it does not intersect the domain boundary. This is purely a statement about
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{1};\rho_{2})
processes whose proof can be skipped on a first reading of the proof of Lemma
3.4
. Next, we will prove in Lemma
3.6
that if we consider an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
process
\eta
on
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
\eta_{-}(\delta)
to
\infty
then provided
\delta>0
is sufficiently small it is very likely that there is a path
\omega
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
0
to the top of the quantum surface disconnected by
\eta
from
\infty
with
0
on its boundary satisfying
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. We will then complete the proof of Lemma
3.4
by using that the law of the part of
\partial\mathcal{D}
on
\eta_{0}
near
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
is comparable to the law of
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
near
0
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose that
D\subseteq\mathbf{C}
is a simply connected domain and
x,y\in\partial D
are distinct. Fix
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}>-2
. Let
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{1};\rho_{2})
process in
D
from
x
to
y
. Let
\tau
(resp.
\overline{\tau}
) be a stopping time (resp. reverse stopping time) for
\eta
. The conditional law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
given
\eta|_{[0,\tau]}
and
\eta|_{[\overline{\tau},\infty)}
on the event that
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
does not intersect
\partial D
is absolutely continuous with respect to that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
from
\eta(\tau)
to
\eta(\overline{\tau})
in the component of
D\setminus(\eta([0,\tau])\cup\eta([\overline{\tau},\infty))
with
\eta(\tau)
on its boundary.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the result in the case that
D=\mathbf{H}
,
x=0
, and
y=\infty
. We can view
\eta
as a flow line of a GFF
h
on
\mathbf{H}
with boundary conditions given by
-\lambda(1+\rho_{1})
on
\mathbf{R}_{-}
and
\lambda(1+\rho_{2})
on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
. Let
\eta_{L}^{\prime}
be the counterflow line of
h+\pi\chi/2
from
\infty
to
0
. Then
\eta
is equal to the right boundary of
\eta_{L}^{\prime}
. Let
\eta_{R}^{\prime}
be the counterflow line of
h-\pi\chi/2
from
\infty
to
0
. Then
\eta
is also equal to the left boundary of
\eta_{R}^{\prime}
. In other words,
\eta
is equal to the common boundary of
\eta_{L}^{\prime}
and
\eta_{R}^{\prime}
.

Let
\tau_{L}
(resp.
\tau_{R}
) be the first time that
\eta_{L}^{\prime}
(resp.
\eta_{R}^{\prime}
) hits
\eta(\overline{\tau})
. Then the common boundary of
\eta_{L}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{L}])
and
\eta_{R}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{R}])
is equal to
\eta([\overline{\tau},\infty))
. It is shown in the proof of
[
25
, Lemma 5.13]
that
A_{\overline{\tau}}=\eta_{L}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{L}])\cup\eta_{R}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{R}])
is a local set for
h
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\eta|_{[0,\tau]}
,
\eta_{L}^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau_{L}]}
,
\eta_{R}^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau_{R}]}
. Then we moreover have that the conditional law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
given
\mathcal{F}_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{1},\tfrac{\kappa}{2}-2-\rho_{1};\rho_{2},\tfrac{\kappa}{2}-2-\rho_{2})
process in the component
D_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
of
\mathbf{H}\setminus(\eta([0,\tau])\cup A_{\overline{\tau}})
with
\eta(\tau)
on its boundary from
\eta(\tau)
to
\eta(\overline{\tau})
. The force points are located at the leftmost and rightmost intersections of
\eta|_{[0,\tau]}
with
\partial\mathbf{H}
and the intersections of
\eta_{L}^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau_{L}]}
and
\eta_{R}^{\prime}|_{[0,\tau_{R}]}
on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
and
\mathbf{R}_{-}
, respectively, which are closest to
0
.

Let
D_{L}
(resp.
D_{R}
) be the component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\eta
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta
with
\eta(\overline{\tau})
on its boundary. Let
\eta_{L}
(resp.
\eta_{R}
) denote the left (resp. right) boundary of
\eta_{L}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{L}])
(resp.
\eta_{R}^{\prime}([0,\tau_{R}])
). Then
\eta_{L}
(resp.
\eta_{R}
) is the flow line of
h
restricted to
D_{L}
(resp.
D_{R}
) starting from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
with angle
\pi
(resp.
-\pi
). In particular, the conditional law of
\eta_{L}
given
\eta
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa/2-2,\rho_{1};-\kappa/2)
in
D_{L}
starting from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
and the conditional law of
\eta_{R}
given
\eta
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(-\kappa/2;\kappa/2-2,\rho_{2})
in
D_{R}
starting from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\eta|_{[0,\tau]}
,
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\tau},\infty)}
,
\eta_{L}
, and
\eta_{R}
. It is moreover shown in
[
25
]
that the conditional law of
\eta
given
\mathcal{G}_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
is the same as the conditional law of
\eta
given
\mathcal{F}_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
.

Fix
\epsilon>0
. Let
\overline{\tau}_{\epsilon}
be the first time
t
that
\eta
gets within distance
\epsilon
of
A_{\overline{\tau}}
. Then it follows that the law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}_{\epsilon}]}
on the event that it does not hit
\partial D
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
D_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\tau)
to
\eta(\overline{\tau})
stopped upon getting within distance
\epsilon
of
A_{\overline{\tau}}
. Since
\eta
hits
A_{\overline{\tau}}
exactly at
\eta(\overline{\tau})
, it follows that if we let
\tau_{\epsilon}
be the first time
t
that
\eta
gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\overline{\tau})
then the law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\tau_{\epsilon}]}
on the event that it does not hit
\partial D
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
D_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\tau)
to
\eta(\overline{\tau})
stopped upon getting within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\overline{\tau})
. Let
U_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
be the component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus(\eta([0,\tau])\cup\eta([\overline{\tau},\infty))
with
\eta(\tau)
on its boundary. Then it follows from
[
17
, Proposition 5.3]
that the law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\tau_{\epsilon}]}
on the event that it does not hit
\partial D
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
U_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\tau)
to
\eta(\overline{\tau})
stopped at the first time it gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\overline{\tau})
.

By reversing the roles of
\tau
and
\overline{\tau}
and using the reversibility of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{1};\rho_{2})
established in
[
25
]
, this implies that the following is true. Let
\overline{\eta}
be the time-reversal of
\eta
and let
\overline{\sigma},\sigma
, respectively, be the times for
\overline{\eta}
which correspond to
\overline{\tau},\tau
, respectively, for
\eta
. Let
\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}
be the first time that
\overline{\eta}
gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\tau)
. Then the law of
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
on the event that it does not hit
\partial D
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
U_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
to
\eta(\tau)
stopped at the first time it gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\tau)
.

For
\epsilon,\delta>0
, we let
E_{\epsilon,\delta}
be the event that
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon},\sigma]}
does not leave
B(\eta(\tau),\delta)
and
\eta_{L},\eta_{R}
do not hit
B(\eta(\tau),2\delta)
. Then for every
\zeta>0
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
so that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
we have that the probability of
E_{\epsilon,\delta}
is at least
1-\zeta
. Let
(\widetilde{\eta},\widetilde{\eta}_{L},\widetilde{\eta}_{R})
be a triple where

(i)

\widetilde{\eta}
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
U_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
to
\eta(\tau)
and

(ii)

the conditional law of
(\widetilde{\eta}_{L},\widetilde{\eta}_{R})
given
\widetilde{\eta}
is the same as the conditional law of
(\eta_{L},\eta_{R})
given
\eta
.

On
E_{\epsilon,\delta}
and the event that
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
does not hit
\partial D
, we have that the joint law of the triple consisting of
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
,
\eta_{L}
, and
\eta_{R}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the triple consisting of
\widetilde{\eta}
stopped upon getting within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\tau)
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{L}
, and
\widetilde{\eta}_{R}
.

Let
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{L}
(resp.
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{R}
) be the first time that
\eta_{L}
(resp.
\eta_{R}
) gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\partial\mathbf{H}
. Then the conditional law of
\eta_{L}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{L}]}
given
\eta
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa/2-2;-\kappa/2)
stopped at the corresponding time. Likewise, the conditional law of
\eta_{R}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{R}]}
given
\eta
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(-\kappa/2;\kappa/2-2)
stopped at the corresponding time. Let
(\widehat{\eta},\widehat{\eta}_{L},\widehat{\eta}_{R})
be a triple where

(i)

\widehat{\eta}
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in
U_{\tau,\overline{\tau}}
from
\eta(\overline{\tau})
to
\eta(\tau)
,

(ii)

the conditional law of
\widehat{\eta}_{L}
given
\widehat{\eta}
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(-\kappa/2;\kappa/2-2)
starting at
\overline{\eta}(\overline{\tau})
, and

(iii)

the conditional law of
\widehat{\eta}_{R}
given
\widehat{\eta}
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa/2-2;-\kappa/2)
starting at
\overline{\eta}(\overline{\tau})
.

On the event that
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
does not hit
\partial\mathbf{H}
, we have that the law of the triple consisting of
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
,
\eta_{L}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{L}]}
, and
\eta_{R}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{R}]}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of the triple consisting of
\widehat{\eta}
stopped at the first time it gets within distance
\epsilon
of
\eta(\tau)
and
\widehat{\eta}_{L},\widehat{\eta}_{R}
both stopped at the first time they get within distance
\epsilon
of
\partial\mathbf{H}
. In particular, on the event that
\overline{\eta}|_{[\overline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma}_{\epsilon}]}
does not hit
\partial\mathbf{H}
, the law of the pair consisting of
\eta_{L}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{L}]}
and
\eta_{R}|_{[0,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{R}]}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of the pair
(\widehat{\eta}_{L},\widehat{\eta}_{R})
both stopped at the corresponding time.

Let
F_{\epsilon,\delta}
be the event that
\eta
does not get within distance
\delta
of
\eta_{L}([\sigma_{\epsilon}^{L},\infty))
or
\eta_{R}([\sigma_{\epsilon}^{R},\infty))
. Then for every
\zeta>0
there exists
\delta_{0}>0
so that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
we have that the probability of
F_{\epsilon,\delta}
is at least
1-\zeta
. On
F_{\epsilon,\delta}
, it follows that the conditional law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
given
\eta_{L}
,
\eta_{R}
and on the event that
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
does not hit
\partial\mathbf{H}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the conditional law of
\widehat{\eta}
given
\widehat{\eta}_{L}
,
\widehat{\eta}_{R}
. Altogether, this implies that the conditional law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
given
\eta_{L}
,
\eta_{R}
and on the event that
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
does not hit
\partial\mathbf{H}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the conditional law of
\widehat{\eta}
given
\widehat{\eta}_{L}
,
\widehat{\eta}_{R}
. Therefore the law of
\eta|_{[\tau,\overline{\tau}]}
on the event that it does not hit
\partial\mathbf{H}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of
\widehat{\eta}
.
∎

Figure 3.3.
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
3.6
; the light blue ball is
B(w_{j},\xi/32)
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose that
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
\eta_{-}(\delta)
to
\infty
which is coupled with
\Gamma_{+}
as a CPI and parameterized by the quantum natural time of its trunk. For each
p,\zeta_{0}\in(0,1)
there exists
\delta_{0},\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
and
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following is true. Let
\mathcal{D}
be the component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus\eta
with
\eta_{+}(0)=0
on its boundary. Let
X_{\epsilon}=\inf_{\omega}{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)
where the infimum is over all paths
\omega
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
contained in
\Upsilon_{+}\cap\mathcal{D}
and which connect
0
to
\eta\cap\partial\mathcal{D}
. Then
\mathbf{P}[X_{\epsilon}\geq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)]\leq\zeta_{0}
.

Proof.

Step 1. Setup.
Fix
p\in(0,1)
. It suffices to show that for every
\zeta_{0}>0
there exists
\xi,\epsilon_{0}>0
so that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the following is true. Let
Y_{\epsilon}
be the Euclidean diameter of the set of points in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
which are connected to
0
by paths
\omega
in
\Upsilon_{+}
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Then
\mathbf{P}[Y_{\epsilon}\leq\xi]\leq\zeta_{0}
. Suppose that this is not true. Then there exists
\zeta_{0}>0
and a sequence
(\epsilon_{n})
of positive numbers with
\epsilon_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
so that
\mathbf{P}[Y_{\epsilon_{n}}\leq 1/n]\geq\zeta_{0}
for every
n\in\mathbf{N}
. We are going to assume that this is true throughout the rest of the proof in order to show that

\mathbf{P}\!\left[\sup_{z,w\in\eta_{+}([0,1])}\mathfrak{d}_{\epsilon_{n}}(z,w;\Gamma_{+})\geq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)\right]\to 1\quad\text{as}\quad n\to\infty.

This, in turn, will contradict the definition of
{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
.

Step 2. First observation.
Fix
\rho_{L},\rho_{R}>-2
and suppose that
\widetilde{\eta}
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{L};\rho_{R})
in
\mathbf{D}
from
-i
to
i
. Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}
be a collection of loops in the components of
\mathbf{D}\setminus\widetilde{\eta}
which are to the left of
\widetilde{\eta}
which in each such component is a conditionally independent
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
. Let
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
be the closure of the union of the loops in
\widetilde{\Gamma}
. Fix
z\in\mathbf{D}
and
r>0
so that
B(z,2r)\subseteq\mathbf{D}
. Then it follows from our assumption above that there exists a sequence
(\epsilon_{n})
of positive numbers with
\epsilon_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
so that the following is true. Let
\widetilde{\sigma}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\widetilde{\eta}(t)\in B(z,r)\}
. On
\widetilde{\sigma}<\infty
, let
\widetilde{Y}_{\epsilon_{n}}
be the Euclidean diameter of the set of points to the left of
\widetilde{\eta}
which are accessible by paths
\omega
in
\widetilde{\Upsilon}
starting from
\widetilde{\eta}(\widetilde{\sigma})
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon_{n}}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Then
\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{Y}_{\epsilon_{n}}\leq 1/n,\ \widetilde{\sigma}<\infty]
is bounded from below by a positive constant which depends only on
\rho_{L}
,
\rho_{R}
,
z
,
r
, and
\zeta_{0}
from Step 1. Indeed, this follows from absolute continuity and our assumption in Step 1.

Step 3. Approximate
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
-metric ball has small Euclidean diameter in many places on the interface.
We are now going to show that the event that the “
\mathfrak{d}_{\epsilon_{n}}(\cdot,\cdot;\Gamma_{+})
-metric ball” at a point along
\eta_{+}
has small diameter is likely to happen at many points. In this step, we are going to focus on proving a version of this statement in the setting of
\mathbf{H}
; in the next step we will subsequently transfer the result to the setting of
\eta_{+}
. To this end, we suppose that
h
is a GFF on
\mathbf{H}
with boundary conditions given by
-\lambda
on
\mathbf{R}_{-}
and
\lambda
on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
. Let
\eta
be the flow line of
h
from
0
to
\infty
. Then
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
curve in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
. Fix
\xi>0
and
\theta\in(0,\pi)
. For each
j
, we let
\tau_{j}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\eta(t)\notin B(0,\xi j)\}
. We then let
\eta_{j,1}
(resp.
\eta_{j,2}
) be the flow line of
h
starting from
\eta_{j}(\tau_{j})
with angle
2\lambda^{\prime}/\chi-\pi
(resp.
-\theta
). Then
\eta_{j,1}
is equal to the right boundary of the counterflow line
\eta_{j,1}^{\prime}
of
h+2\lambda^{\prime}-\pi\chi/2
from
\infty
to
\eta(\tau_{j})
. We note that the angle difference between
\eta_{j,1}
and
\eta_{j,2}
is equal to
2\lambda^{\prime}/\chi-\pi+\theta
and that flow lines intersect whenever the angle difference is between
0
and
2\lambda^{\prime}/\chi
. That is, with any choice of
\theta\in(0,\pi)
we have that
\eta_{j,1}
,
\eta_{j,2}
a.s. intersect each other.

Given
\eta
, we let
\Gamma_{L}
be a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
in the component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\eta
which is to the left of
\eta
. We assume that
\Gamma_{L}
is generated from
h
using the coupling of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa-6)
with the GFF so that
\eta_{j,1}^{\prime}
is a CPI in
\Gamma_{L}
for each
j
. Then the conditional law of
\Gamma_{L}
in any component
U
of
\mathbf{H}\setminus(\eta\cup\eta_{1,j})
which is to the left of
\eta
and to the right of
\eta_{1,j}
is that of a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
in
U
.

For each
j\in\mathbf{N}
and
k\in\{1,2\}
, we let
\tau_{j,k}
be the first time that
\eta_{j,k}
leaves
B(\eta_{j}(\tau_{j}),\xi/16)
. We let
\mathcal{F}_{j}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\eta|_{[0,\tau_{j}]}
and
\eta_{i,k}|_{[0,\tau_{i,k}]}
for
1\leq i\leq j
and
k\in\{1,2\}
. We also let
E_{j}
be the event that:

(i)

There exists
w\in\mathbf{H}
so that
\eta_{j,1}|_{[0,\tau_{j,1}]}
,
\eta_{j,2}|_{[0,\tau_{j,2}]}
do not intersect
B(w,\xi/32)
and disconnect
B(w,\xi/32)
from
\infty
and the harmonic measure of
\eta_{j,k}([0,\tau_{j,k}])
in
\mathbf{H}\setminus\cup_{k=1}^{2}\eta_{j,k}([0,\tau_{j,k}])
as seen from
w
is at least
1/4
for
k\in\{1,2\}
.

(ii)

Let
V_{j}
be the component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus\cup_{k=1}^{2}\eta_{j,k}([0,\tau_{j,k}])
disconnected from
\infty
as in (
i
) which is first visited by
\eta_{j,1}
. Let
w_{j}
be the point
w
as in (
i
) with the smallest real part, breaking ties by taking the point with smallest imaginary part. Let
\Gamma_{j,L}
be the loops of
\Gamma_{L}
which are contained in
V_{j}
. Let
\sigma_{j}
be the first time that
\eta
visits
B(w_{j},\xi/64)
. Then
\sigma_{j}<\infty
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\epsilon_{n}}(\eta(\sigma_{j}),\partial B(w_{j},\xi/32);\Gamma_{j,L})\geq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
.

We claim that there exists
p_{0}>0
which does not depend on
j
so that

(3.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[E_{j}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]\geq p_{0}\quad\text{for each}\quad j\in\mathbf{N}.

To start to prove (
3.1
), for each
j
we let
z_{j}
be the point on
\mathbf{H}\cap\partial B(0,\xi j)
with
\arg(z_{j})=((\mathrm{Im}(\eta(\tau_{j-1}))\wedge(\pi-\xi))\vee\xi
. Let
A_{j}
be the union of
\eta([0,\tau_{j}])
and
\eta_{i,k}([0,\tau_{i,k}])
for
1\leq i\leq j
and
k\in\{1,2\}
. Let
\varphi_{j}
be the unique conformal map from the unbounded component of
\mathbf{H}\setminus A_{j-1}
to
\mathbf{H}
which fixes
\infty
and takes
z_{j}
to
i
. Then there exists a constant
M>0
so that
\varphi(\eta(\tau_{j-1}))
is contained in
[-M,M]
(as the harmonic measure in
\mathbf{H}\setminus\eta([0,\tau_{j-1}])
as seen from
z_{j}
of the left side of
\eta
and
\mathbf{R}_{-}
is bounded from below and the same is also true for the and right side of
\eta
and
\mathbf{R}_{+}
).
[
36
, Lemmas 2.3–2.5]
thus imply that there exists
p_{0}>0
which does not depend on
j
such that the conditional probability given
\mathcal{F}_{j-1}
of part (
i
) of
E_{j}
is at least
p_{0}
.

We are now going to show that, by possibly decreasing the value of
p_{0}>0
, we have that (
3.1
) holds. Let
w_{j}
,
V_{j}
be as in (
ii
). Let
\psi_{j}
be the unique conformal transformation
V_{j}\to\mathbf{D}
which takes the first (resp. last) point on
\partial V
visited by
\eta
to
-i
(resp.
i
) and is such that
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{j}(w_{j}))=0
. Then there exists
\rho_{L},\rho_{R}>-2
so that
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}=\psi_{j}(\eta)
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\rho_{L};\rho_{R})
in
\mathbf{D}
from
-i
to
i
. Since the harmonic measure of the clockwise (resp. counterclockwise) arc of
\partial\mathbf{D}
from
-i
to
i
as seen from
\widetilde{w}_{j}=\psi_{j}(w_{j})
is at least
1/4
, it follows that there exists
r_{0}>0
so that
B(\widetilde{w}_{j},2r_{0})\subseteq\mathbf{D}
. Let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}
be the first time that
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
visits
B(\widetilde{w}_{j},r_{0})
. Let
\widetilde{Y}_{\epsilon_{n}}
be the Euclidean diameter of the set of points to the left of
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}
which are accessible by paths in
\omega
in
\psi_{j}(\Gamma_{j,L})
starting from
\widetilde{\eta}_{j}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon_{n}}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Step 2 implies that there exists
q_{0}>0
so that
\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{Y}_{\epsilon_{n}}\leq 1/n,\ \widetilde{\sigma}_{j}<\infty]\geq q_{0}
. We note that the restriction of
(\psi_{j}^{-1})^{\prime}
to
\psi_{j}(B(w_{j},\xi/32))
is bounded from below by a constant times
\xi
. Therefore (
3.1
) follows by conformally mapping back.

Step 4. Contradiction to the definition of
{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
.
Suppose that we have the setup described in Step 3. Fix
N\in\mathbf{N}
and let
F_{N}=\cup_{j=1}^{N}E_{j}
be the event that
E_{j}
occurs for some
1\leq j\leq N
. It follows from (
3.1
) that
\mathbf{P}[F_{N}]\to 1
as
N\to\infty
. Let
K_{\xi}=[-\xi^{1/2},\xi^{1/2}]\times[\xi/128,\xi^{1/2}]
and let
G_{N}
be the event that
E_{j}
occurs and
\eta(\tau_{j})\in K_{\xi}
. Then it further follows that for each
p_{0}\in(0,1)
we can choose
\xi>0
sufficiently small so that with
N=\xi^{-1/2}
we have that
\mathbf{P}[G_{N}]\geq p_{0}
. Let
\varphi
be the unique conformal transformation from
\mathbf{H}
to
\mathbf{C}\setminus\eta_{-}
which fixes
0
and
\infty
and takes
-1
to the prime end corresponding to the point
\eta_{-}(1)
which is on
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
. Then there exists a constant
c\geq 1
depending only on
\eta_{-}
so that the restriction of
|\varphi^{\prime}|
to
K_{\xi}
is between
c^{-1}\xi^{-1}
and
c\xi^{-1/2}
. We assume that
\eta_{+}=\varphi(\eta)
, modulo parameterization. It therefore follows that on
G_{N}
we have that the “
\mathfrak{d}_{\epsilon_{n}}(\cdot,\cdot;\Gamma_{+})
-metric ball” centered at
0
of radius
{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
does not contain all of the image under
\varphi
of
\eta
up until time
\tau_{N}
. As the probability that
\varphi(\eta([0,\tau_{N}]))
is contained in
\eta_{+}([0,1])
(recall that
\eta_{+}
is parameterized by quantum length) tends to
1
as
\xi\to 0
, the desired contradiction to the definition of
{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon_{n}}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

In order to start to prove the lemma, we will first explain why a simpler version of the statement holds. Let
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{-1}(\kappa-6)
process from
0
to
\infty
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
coupled as a CPI of
\Gamma_{+}
. Let
X
be an exponential random variable with mean
1
which is independent of everything else and let
\mathcal{D}_{0}
be the component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus\eta
with
\eta_{+}(X\delta)
on its boundary. It follows from the argument used to prove Lemma
3.6
that for each
\zeta_{0}\in(0,1)
there exists
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
so that for each
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
so that for every
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0})
the probability that there is a path
\omega
in
\Upsilon_{+}
from
\eta_{+}(X\delta)
to the part of
\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}
which is contained in
\eta
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
is at least
1-\zeta_{0}
.

Let now describe the law of the ensemble of paths which make up
\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}
. We know that the conditional law of
\eta_{+}
given
\eta_{-}
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
curve in
\mathbf{C}\setminus\eta_{-}
from
0
to
\infty
. Let
\eta_{R}
be the right boundary of
\eta
. Note that
\eta_{R}
is also the right boundary of the trunk of
\eta
. Recall that the trunk is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\kappa^{\prime}-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
0
to
\infty
with its force point located at
0^{-}
. We thus know from
[
24
, Theorem 1.5]
that the law of
\eta_{R}
is equal to that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa-4;2-\kappa)
process from
\infty
to
0
in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
. Also, note that
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is the component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus\eta_{R}
with
\eta_{+}(X\delta)
on its boundary.

We are now going to make a comparison between the law of
\mathcal{D}
as defined in the lemma statement to the law of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
as described just above. Let
L
be the quantum length of
\eta_{0}
. Let
G
be the event that
\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cap\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}])=\emptyset
and the part of
\eta_{0}
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
, i.e.,
\eta_{0}|_{[X_{1},L-Y_{1}]}
does not hit
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
. On
G
, Lemma
3.5
implies that the conditional law of
\eta_{0}|_{[X_{1},L-Y_{1}]}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}
in the unbounded component
U
of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus(\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cup\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}]))
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
. We also have that the law of
\eta_{1}
given
\eta_{0}
is that of an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}(\kappa-4;2-\kappa)
from
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
to
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
.

The previous two paragraphs imply that the following is true. Let
\varphi
be the unique conformal transformation from
\mathbf{C}\setminus\eta_{-}
to
U
which takes
0
to
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
and
\infty
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
. Then it follows from the above that the law of the pair
(\eta_{0},\eta_{1})
on
G
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of the pair
(\varphi(\eta_{+}),\varphi(\eta_{R}))
. Moreover, since
\eta_{+}(X\delta)
has a positive chance of being in the boundary of any fixed component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus\eta
which intersects
\eta_{+}
, it follows that the law of
\mathcal{D}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the law of
\varphi(\mathcal{D}_{0})
. The result then follows since we know that
|\varphi^{\prime}|
is a.s. bounded on a neighborhood of the closure of
\mathcal{D}_{0}
(recall (
1.1
)).
∎

3.4.
Proofs of main crossing statements

Figure 3.4.
Illustration of the proof of Lemma
3.1
. Shown are just the left boundaries of
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
(
\eta_{0}
, dark blue) as viewed from
\eta_{+}(Y_{0})
and
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
(
\eta_{1}
, dark green) as viewed from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
, using the notation from Lemma
3.4
. Proposition
3.3
implies that on the event that
I\cap\Upsilon\neq\emptyset
, it is a positive conditional probability event given
\mathcal{N}
that a path in
\mathcal{D}
from
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
to
\eta_{1}
in
\Upsilon
passes through
I
and
\eta_{1}(Z_{1})\in B(z_{R},\zeta_{0}d_{0})
, as shown.

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

See Figure
3.4
for an illustration of the argument. Let
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{+}
from
0
to
\infty
coupled with
\Gamma_{+}
as a CPI. We assume that
\eta
is parameterized according to the quantum natural time of its trunk. Let
\tau_{\delta}=\inf\{t\geq\delta^{4/\kappa}:\eta(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}\}
and let
\mathcal{N}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the region disconnected from
\infty
by
\eta([0,\tau_{\delta}])
. Let also
d_{0}=\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{N})
.

Let
\widetilde{\eta}_{0}
,
\widetilde{\eta}_{1}
,
\eta_{0}
,
\eta_{1}
,
X_{0}
,
Y_{0}
,
X_{1}
,
Y_{1}
,
Z_{1}
be as in the statement of Lemma
3.4
, taken to be conditionally independent of everything else given
\Gamma_{+}
. On
\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cap\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}])=\emptyset
let also
\mathcal{D}
be the component of
\mathcal{H}_{+}\setminus(\eta_{0}\cup\eta_{1})
between
\eta_{0}
and
\eta_{1}
with
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
on its boundary as in Lemma
3.4
. Let
Q
be the event that
\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cap\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}])\neq\emptyset
, the part of
\eta_{0}
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
does not hit
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
, and there does not exist a path
\omega
in
\Upsilon_{+}\cap\mathcal{D}
which connects
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
to
\eta_{1}
with
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\leq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Fix
\zeta_{1}>0
and assume that we have chosen
\delta>0
sufficiently small so that Lemma
3.4
implies that
\mathbf{P}[Q]\leq\zeta_{1}
.

Let
Z
be chosen uniformly from the boundary measure on the top of
\mathcal{N}
and let
I
(resp.
\widetilde{I}
) be the interval of quantum length
\zeta_{0}\delta
(resp.
3\zeta_{0}\delta/2
) on the top of
\mathcal{N}
centered at
Z
. Let
E=\{I\cap\Upsilon_{+}\neq\emptyset\}
. Let
F
be the event that
\eta_{0}([0,X_{1}])\cap\overline{\eta}_{0}([0,Y_{1}])=\emptyset
, the part of
\eta_{0}
from
\eta_{0}(X_{1})
to
\overline{\eta}_{0}(Y_{1})
does not hit
\partial\mathcal{H}_{+}
, and every path in
\Upsilon_{+}\cap\mathcal{D}
from
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})
to
\eta_{1}
passes through
I
,
\eta_{0}(Z_{1})\in B(z_{R},\zeta_{0}d_{0})
, and
\partial\mathcal{D}
has distance at least
\xi_{0}d_{0}
from
\partial\mathcal{N}\setminus\widetilde{I}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\Gamma_{+}
,
\mathcal{N}
, and
Z
. Note that
E
is
\mathcal{F}
-measurable. It follows from Proposition
3.3
that

\mathbf{P}\big[\mathbf{P}[F\,|\,\mathcal{F}]>0\,|\,E\big]\to 1\quad\text{as}\quad\xi_{0}\to 0.

Fix
p_{0}>0
and let
G=\{\mathbf{P}[F\,|\,\mathcal{F}]{\bf 1}_{E}\geq p_{0}{\bf 1}_{E}\}
. Fix
\zeta_{2}>0
. By making
p_{0},\xi_{0}>0
sufficiently small we have that
\mathbf{P}[G^{c}\cap E]\leq\zeta_{2}
. We note that by scale invariance the choice of
p_{0}
does not depend on
\delta
. Therefore we can take
\zeta_{1}=p_{0}\zeta_{2}
and assume that
\delta>0
is sufficiently small so that
\mathbf{P}[Q]\leq\zeta_{1}
.

Let
H
be the event that
E
occurs and every path
\omega
in
\Upsilon_{+}\cap\mathcal{N}
connecting
B(z_{R},\zeta_{0}d_{0})
to
\widetilde{I}
with distance at least
\xi_{0}d_{0}
from
\partial\mathcal{N}\setminus\widetilde{I}
satisfies
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\geq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. Then we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}[H]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}[{\bf 1}_{E}{\bf 1}_{H}]\leq\frac{1}{p_{0}}\mathbf{E}[{\bf 1}_{E}{\bf 1}_{H}\mathbf{P}[F\,|\,\mathcal{F}]]+\mathbf{P}[G^{c}\cap E]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{0}}\mathbf{P}[E,\ H,\ F]+\mathbf{P}[G^{c}\cap E]\quad\text{($E,H\in\mathcal{F}$)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p_{0}}\mathbf{P}[Q]+\mathbf{P}[G^{c}]\leq\frac{\zeta_{1}}{p_{0}}+\zeta_{2}\leq 2\zeta_{2}.

Consider the event that there exists
J
on the top of
\partial\mathcal{N}
with quantum length
\zeta_{0}
, intersects
\Upsilon_{+}
, and such that if
\widetilde{J}
is the interval on the top of
\partial\mathcal{N}
with the same center as
J
but twice the quantum length every path
\omega
from
B(z_{R},\zeta_{0}d_{0})
to
\widetilde{J}
in
\Upsilon_{+}\cap\mathcal{N}
and with distance at least
\xi_{0}d_{0}
to
\partial\mathcal{N}\setminus\widetilde{J}
satisfies
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\geq{{\mathfrak{q}}}_{\epsilon}^{\mathrm{HP}}(p)
. On this event, there is a positive chance that
\widetilde{I}\subseteq\widetilde{J}
. Therefore the probability of this event is at most a constant times
\zeta_{2}
, from which the result follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

This follows from the same argument used to prove Lemma
3.1
.
∎

4.
Percolation exploration

The purpose of this section is to describe a type of supercritical percolation exploration inside of a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
carpet drawn on top of a quantum half-plane. In particular, we will decompose the
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
carpet using “chunks” of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
processes coupled as CPIs. Our ultimate aim is to show that two points on the boundary of the quantum half-plane are likely to be connected by a collection of such chunks which are in a certain sense “good”. The first step in this (Section
4.1
) is to define a version of the exploration where we start with chunks which each consist of order
2^{-K}
units of quantum natural time where
K\in\mathbf{N}
large, with the chunk size growing larger until we see chunks with of order
2^{-J}
units of quantum natural time where
J\in\mathbf{N}
with
J\leq K
is fixed, and then getting smaller with chunks with quantum natural time tending to
0
. One can think of the chunks in this path as being analogous to a path between two boundary points on
\partial\mathbf{H}
which passes through at most a fixed number of squares of each scale in a Whitney cube decomposition of
\mathbf{H}
. We will then explain in Section
4.2
that it is likely that this exploration “succeeds” in the sense that it is likely that we can construct a path of such chunks all of which satisfy an event which allows us to say that the chunk is “good”. Finally, in Section
4.3
we will explain how one can take a (subsequential) limit as
K\to\infty
to construct an exploration which starts with arbitrarily small chunks and connects two boundary points.

4.1.
Definition of the exploration

Suppose that
\mathcal{H}=(\mathbf{H},h,0,\infty)
is a quantum half-plane. Suppose that we have fixed
\delta_{0},a_{0}\in(0,1)
and
\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}\in(1-\kappa/4,1)
. Let
x_{-1}<0<x_{1}
be such that
\nu_{h}([x_{-1},0])=\nu_{h}([0,x_{1}])=1
. We assume that
\Gamma
is a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
on
\mathbf{H}
. We will describe below an adaptive exploration of
\Gamma
and
\mathcal{H}
. We will assume that the various
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves in what follows are coupled with
\Gamma
as CPIs. Suppose that
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
in
\mathbf{H}
from
0
to
\infty
which is parameterized by the quantum natural time of its trunk,
\mathcal{N}_{t}
is the quantum surface parameterized by the domain disconnected by
\eta|_{[0,t]}
from
\infty
, and
\sigma
is a stopping time for the filtration generated by
\mathcal{N}_{t}
. Then the
top
(resp.
bottom
) of
\mathcal{N}_{t}
is
\partial\mathcal{N}_{t}\cap\mathbf{H}
(resp.
\partial\mathcal{N}_{t}\cap\partial\mathbf{H}
).

Figure 4.1.
Illustration of the first few chunks in the percolation exploration of a
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
decorated quantum half-plane. The chunks which are “good” are indicated in green and the chunks which are “bad” are indicated in red. Part of our definition of a chunk being good is that the quantum surface it disconnects from
\infty
is simply connected, which is why the green chunks are homeomorphic to
\mathbf{D}
while the bad chunks are not (there are also other ways in which a chunk can be bad). The arrows indicate the starting points of the
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
’s which cut out the chunks and the squares indicate the points on the tops of the good chunks whose clockwise boundary length distance to the wedge boundary in which they are drawn is an integer multiple of
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
; we always take chunks to start at such points.

4.1.1.
Increasing chunk sizes

Fix
J,K\in\mathbf{N}
with
J\leq K
. We consider the following exploration of
\mathcal{H}
by
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves. Let
\eta_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
be an independent
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curve in
\mathcal{H}
from
0
to
\infty
and let
\sigma_{1,K}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-K}\delta_{0}:\eta_{1,K}^{\uparrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}\}\wedge 2^{-K}
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface disconnected from
\infty
by
\eta_{1,K}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{1,K}^{\uparrow}])
and let
\mathcal{T}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the top of
\mathcal{N}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
. We also let
\mathcal{F}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the quantum surface
\mathcal{N}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
decorated by the path
\eta_{1,K}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{1,K}^{\uparrow}]}
and let
E_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{1,K}^{\uparrow}
-measurable event. We then inductively define
\sigma
-algebras
\mathcal{F}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
, quantum half-planes
\mathcal{H}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
,
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
, events
E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
, stopping times
\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
, and quantum surfaces
\mathcal{N}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
with tops
\mathcal{T}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
as follows. We let
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded component of
\mathcal{H}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}\setminus\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}])
and marked by the points
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}(\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow})
and
\infty
. If
E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
occurs, we let
\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
starting from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
. For
1\leq i\leq j
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}
be the set of points in
\mathcal{T}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}
whose clockwise boundary length distance to
\partial\mathcal{H}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}
is an integer multiple of
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
. If
E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
does not occur, we let
\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
starting from the rightmost point
z_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
on
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
which is:

•

to the left of the leftmost point of
\mathcal{T}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
,

•

in
\mathcal{X}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}
with
i<j
such that the part of
\mathcal{T}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}\cap\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
to the left of
z_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
has quantum length at least
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
and with boundary length distance at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
from
\Upsilon_{+}
.

In the case that there is no such point, we take the starting point of
\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
to be
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}(\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow})
. We let
\sigma_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-K}\delta_{0}:\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}\}\wedge 2^{-K}
, let
\mathcal{N}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
disconnected by
\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}])
from
\infty
,
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{i=1}^{j+1}\mathcal{N}_{i,K}^{\uparrow}))
and decorated by the paths
\eta_{i,K}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{i,K}^{\uparrow}]}
for
1\leq i\leq j+1
. Finally, we let
E_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
-measurable event. Let
n_{K}^{\uparrow}
be the first
j
so that either

•

x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
, or

•

E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
occurs and the boundary length distance along
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}
from
\eta_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}(0)
to
x_{1}
is at most
1-2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
.

Suppose that we have defined the exploration procedure for some
J+1\leq M\leq K
and we are on the event that
x_{1}
has not been disconnected from
\infty
. We then define it for
M-1
as follows. Let
\eta_{1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be an independent
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curve in
\mathcal{H}_{1,M-1}^{\uparrow}=\mathcal{H}_{n_{M}^{\uparrow}+1,M}^{\uparrow}
from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{n_{M},M}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{n_{M},M}^{\uparrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{n_{M},M}^{\uparrow}
to
\infty
. Let
\sigma_{1,M-1}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-(M-1)}\delta_{0}:\eta_{1,M-1}^{\uparrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{1,M-1}^{\uparrow}\}\wedge 2^{-(M-1)}
. We then inductively define quantum half-planes
\mathcal{H}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
,
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves
\eta_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
, events
E_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
, stopping times
\sigma_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
, and quantum surfaces
\mathcal{N}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
with tops
\mathcal{T}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
as follows. We let
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded component of
\mathcal{H}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}\setminus\eta_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}])
and marked by the points
\eta_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}(\sigma_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow})
and
\infty
. If
E_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
occurs, we let
\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
starting from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
. For
1\leq i\leq j
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be the set of points in
\mathcal{T}_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}
whose clockwise boundary length distance to
\partial\mathcal{H}_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}
is an integer multiple of
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)(M-1)}
. If
E_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
does not occur, we let
\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
starting from the rightmost point
z_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
on
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
which is:

•

to the left of the leftmost point of
\mathcal{T}_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
and

•

in
\mathcal{X}_{i,N}^{\uparrow}
with
M-1\leq N\leq K
such that the part of
\mathcal{T}_{i,N}^{\uparrow}\cap\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,N}^{\uparrow}
to the left of
z_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
has quantum length at least
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
and with boundary length distance at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
from
\Upsilon_{+}
.

In the case that there is no such point, we take the starting point of
\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
to be
\eta_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}(\sigma_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow})
. We let
\sigma_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-(M-1)}\delta_{0}:\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}\}\wedge 2^{-(M-1)}
, let
\mathcal{N}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
disconnected by
\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}])
from
\infty
,
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\mathcal{F}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
and the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{i=1}^{j+1}\mathcal{N}_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}))
and decorated by the paths
\eta_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{i,M-1}^{\uparrow}]}
for
1\leq i\leq j+1
. Finally, we let
E_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
-measurable event. Let
n_{M-1}^{\uparrow}
be the first
j
so that either

•

x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
, or

•

E_{j,M-1}^{\uparrow}
occurs and the boundary length distance in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}
from
\eta_{j+1,M-1}^{\uparrow}(0)
to
x_{1}
is at most
1-2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)(M-1)}
.

4.1.2.
Decreasing chunk sizes

We will now define the continuation of the exploration after scale
J
as above. The definition will be similar except the chunk sizes will be decreasing (rather than increasing) as the exploration gets closer to
x_{1}
.

Let
\eta_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
be an independent
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curve in
\mathcal{H}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}=\mathcal{H}_{n_{J}^{\uparrow}+1,J}^{\uparrow}
from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{n_{J},J}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{n_{J},J}^{\uparrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{n_{J},J}^{\uparrow}
to
\infty
. Let
\sigma_{1,J}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-J}\delta_{0}:\eta_{1,J}^{\downarrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}\}\wedge 2^{-J}
. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1,J}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum surface disconnected from
\infty
by
\eta_{1,J}^{\uparrow}([0,\sigma_{1,J}^{\uparrow}])
and let
\mathcal{T}_{1,J}^{\uparrow}
be the top of
\mathcal{N}_{1,J}^{\uparrow}
. We also let
\mathcal{F}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\mathcal{F}_{n_{J}^{\uparrow},J}^{\uparrow}
and the quantum surface
\mathcal{N}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
decorated by the path
\eta_{1,J}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{1,J}^{\downarrow}]}
and let
E_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
-measurable event. We then inductively define
\sigma
-algebras
\mathcal{F}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
, quantum half-planes
\mathcal{H}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
,
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves
\eta_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
, events
E_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
, stopping times
\sigma_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
, and quantum surfaces
\mathcal{N}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
with tops
\mathcal{T}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
as follows. We let
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded component of
\mathcal{H}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\eta_{j,J}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j,J}^{\downarrow}])
and marked by the points
\eta_{j,J}^{\downarrow}(\sigma_{j,J}^{\downarrow})
and
\infty
. If
E_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
occurs, we let
\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
starting from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{j,J}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j,J}^{\downarrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
. For
1\leq i\leq j
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i,J}^{\downarrow}
be the set of points in
\mathcal{T}_{i,J}^{\downarrow}
whose clockwise boundary length distance to
\partial\mathcal{H}_{i,J}^{\downarrow}
is an integer multiple of
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)J}
. If
E_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
does not occur, we let
\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
starting from the rightmost point
z_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
on
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
which is:

•

to the left of the leftmost point of
\mathcal{T}_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
and

•

in
\mathcal{X}_{i,N}^{\bullet}
for
\bullet\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}
where
K\leq N\leq J
(
\bullet=\uparrow
) or
N=J
(
\bullet=\downarrow
) such that the part of
\mathcal{T}_{i,N}^{\bullet}\cap\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
to the left of
z_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
has quantum length at least
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
and with boundary length distance at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
from
\Upsilon_{+}
.

In the case that there is no such point, we take the starting point of
\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
to be
\eta_{j,J}^{\downarrow}(\sigma_{j,J}^{\downarrow})
. We let
\sigma_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-J}\delta_{0}:\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}\}\wedge 2^{-J}
, let
\mathcal{N}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
be the quantum surface in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
disconnected by
\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}])
from
\infty
,
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\mathcal{F}_{1,J}^{\downarrow}
and the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{i=1}^{j+1}\mathcal{N}_{i,J}^{\downarrow}))
and decorated by the paths
\eta_{i,J}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{i,J}^{\downarrow}]}
for
1\leq i\leq j+1
. Finally, we let
E_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
-measurable event. Let
n_{J}^{\downarrow}
be the first
j
so that either

•

x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
, or

•

E_{j,J}^{\downarrow}
occurs and the boundary length distance in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}
from
\eta_{j+1,J}^{\downarrow}(0)
to
x_{1}
is at most
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)J}
.

Suppose that we have defined the downward exploration procedure for some
M\geq J
. We then define it for
M+1
as follows. Let
\eta_{1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be an independent
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curve in
\mathcal{H}_{1,M+1}^{\downarrow}=\mathcal{H}_{n_{M}^{\downarrow}+1,M}^{\downarrow}
from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{j,M}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\downarrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
to
\infty
. Let
\sigma_{1,M+1}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-(M+1)}\delta_{0}:\eta_{1,M+1}^{\downarrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{1,M+1}^{\downarrow}\}\wedge 2^{-(M+1)}
. We then inductively define quantum half-planes
\mathcal{H}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
,
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
curves
\eta_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
, events
E_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
, stopping times
\sigma_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
, and quantum surfaces
\mathcal{N}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
with tops
\mathcal{T}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
as follows. We let
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be the quantum surface parameterized by the unbounded component of
\mathcal{H}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}\setminus\eta_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}])
and marked by the points
\eta_{j,M+1}^{\uparrow}(\sigma_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow})
and
\infty
. If
E_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
occurs, we let
\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
starting from the rightmost intersection of
\eta_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}])
with
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
. For
1\leq i\leq j
, let
\mathcal{X}_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be the set of points in
\mathcal{T}_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}
whose clockwise boundary length distance to
\partial\mathcal{H}_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}
is an integer multiple of
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)(M+1)}
. If
E_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
does not occur, we let
\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
starting from the rightmost point
z_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
on
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
which is:

•

to the left of the leftmost point of
\mathcal{T}_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
and

•

in
\mathcal{X}_{i,N}^{\bullet}
for
\bullet\in\{\uparrow,\downarrow\}
where
K\leq N\leq J
(
\bullet=\uparrow
) or
M+1\leq N\leq J
(
\bullet=\downarrow
) such that the part of
\mathcal{T}_{i,N}^{\bullet}\cap\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
to the left of
z_{j,M+1}^{\downarrow}
has quantum length at least
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
and with boundary length distance at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)N}
from
\Upsilon_{+}
.

In the case that there is no such point, we take the starting point of
\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
to be
\eta_{j,M}^{\downarrow}(\sigma_{j,M}^{\downarrow})
. We let
\sigma_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}=\inf\{t\geq 2^{-(M+1)}\delta_{0}:\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}(t)\in\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}\}\wedge 2^{-(M+1)}
, let
\mathcal{N}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be the quantum surface in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
disconnected by
\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}([0,\sigma_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}])
from
\infty
,
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,M}^{\downarrow}
and the quantum surface parameterized by
\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{cl}(\cup_{i=1}^{j+1}\mathcal{N}_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}))
and decorated by the paths
\eta_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{i,M+1}^{\downarrow}]}
for
1\leq i\leq j+1
. Finally, we let
E_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
-measurable event. Let
n_{M+1}^{\downarrow}
be the first
j
so that either

•

x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
, or

•

E_{j,M-1}^{\downarrow}
occurs and the boundary length distance in
\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}
from
\eta_{j+1,M+1}^{\downarrow}(0)
to
x_{1}
is at most
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)(M+1)}
.

4.1.3.
Definition of events

Figure 4.2.
Illustration of two of the ways in which the exploration can fail. The blue region illustrates the quantum surface parameterized by the exploration before discovering the chunk which led to the failure. On the left, the exploration failed due to a large downward jump (which also disconnected
x_{1}
from
\infty
. On the right, the exploration failed due to a large upward jump (corresponding to the discovery of a large
{\rm CLE}_{\kappa}
loop).

Fix
c_{F}>1
; we will choose its value later. We let
F_{K}^{\uparrow}
be the event that

•

x_{-1}
or
x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
by one of
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}]}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{K}^{\uparrow}
,

•

there exists
1\leq j\leq n_{K}^{\uparrow}
so that
\eta_{j,K}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}]}
makes a jump (downward or upward) of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
, or

•

n_{K}^{\uparrow}\geq c_{F}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}
.

For each
J\leq M\leq K-1
, we let
F_{M}^{\uparrow}
be the event that

•

x_{-1}
or
x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
by one of
\eta_{j,M}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\uparrow}]}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\uparrow}
,

•

there exists
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\uparrow}
so that
\eta_{j,M}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\uparrow}]}
makes a jump (downward or upward) of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)M}
,

•

n_{M}^{\uparrow}\geq c_{F}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}
, or

•

the curves
\eta_{j,M}^{\uparrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\uparrow}]}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\uparrow}
disconnect all of the tops
\mathcal{T}_{j,M+1}^{\uparrow}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M+1}^{\uparrow}
from
\infty
.

For each
J\leq M
, we let
F_{M}^{\downarrow}
be the event that

•

x_{-1}
or
x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
by one of
\eta_{j,M}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\downarrow}]}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\downarrow}
,

•

there exists
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\downarrow}
so that
\eta_{j,M}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\downarrow}]}
makes a jump (downward or upward) of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)M}
,

•

n_{M}^{\downarrow}\geq c_{F}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}
(
M\geq J+1
) or
n_{M}^{\downarrow}\geq c_{F}2^{(\kappa/4)M}
(
M=J
), or

•

the curves
\eta_{j,M}^{\downarrow}|_{[0,\sigma_{j,M}^{\downarrow}]}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M}^{\downarrow}
disconnect all of the tops

–

(if
M\geq J+1
)
\mathcal{T}_{j,M-1}^{\downarrow}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{M-1}^{\downarrow}

–

(if
M=J
)
\mathcal{T}_{j,J}^{\uparrow}
for
1\leq j\leq n_{J}^{\uparrow}

from
\infty
.

We say that the exploration
fails
if one of
F_{M}^{\uparrow}
for
J\leq M\leq K
or
F_{M}^{\downarrow}
for
J\leq M
occurs.

4.2.
Estimates

Lemma 4.1
.

Fix
C_{0}>0
. There exist constants
c,\delta_{0},a_{0}\in(0,1)
and
c_{F}>0
so that with
p_{0}=1-a_{0}
the following is true for every
J\in\mathbf{N}
. Assume that

(i)

\mathbf{P}[E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,M}^{\uparrow}]\geq p_{0}
for all
j\geq 1
and
J\leq M\leq K
,

(ii)

\mathbf{P}[E_{j,M}^{\downarrow}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,M}^{\downarrow}]\geq p_{0}
for all
j\geq 1
and
J\leq M
and

(iii)

E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
(resp.
E_{j,M}^{\downarrow}
) implies that the top boundary length of
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\downarrow}
) is at most
C_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M}
for all
j\geq 1
and
J\leq M\leq K
(resp.
J\leq M
).

Then the probability that the exploration as defined in Section
4.1
fails is
O(2^{-cJ})
.

Proof.

We are going to show that the following is true. There exists a constant
c>0
so that for each
J\leq M\leq K
we have
\mathbf{P}[F_{M}^{\uparrow}]=O(2^{-cM})
and for
M\geq J
we have
\mathbf{P}[F_{M}^{\downarrow}]=O(2^{-cM})
. Then the result follows by applying a union bound. We will explain carefully how to obtain the upper bound for
\mathbf{P}[F_{K}^{\uparrow}]
and then subsequently explain the necessary modifications to bound
\mathbf{P}[F_{M}^{\uparrow}]
for
J\leq M\leq K
and
\mathbf{P}[F_{M}^{\downarrow}]
for
J\leq M
.

Step 1. Definition of boundary length processes.
For each
j
and
M
, we let
T_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
denote the quantum length of the top
\mathcal{T}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
of
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
. We also let
B_{L,j,M}^{\uparrow}
(resp.
B_{R,j,M}^{\uparrow}
) denote the quantum length of the part of the bottom of
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
which is to the left (resp. right) of
\eta_{j,M}^{\uparrow}(0)
. Finally, we define
L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
(resp.
R_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
) to be equal to the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\setminus\partial\mathcal{H}
to the left (resp. right) of
\eta_{j,M}^{\uparrow}(0)
minus the quantum length of the part of
\partial\mathcal{H}\setminus\partial\mathcal{H}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
to the left (resp. right) of
0
. We define
L_{0,M}^{\uparrow}=L_{n_{M-1},M-1}^{\uparrow}
and
R_{0,M}^{\uparrow}=R_{n_{M-1},M-1}^{\uparrow}
. On
(E_{j,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}
, we let
S_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
be the quantum length of the interval on
\partial\mathcal{H}_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}
from
\eta_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}(0)
to the leftmost point on the top of
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
. For each
j\geq 0
, we then have that

(4.1)

L_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}-L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}=(T_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-B_{L,j,M}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}}-(B_{L,j,M}^{\uparrow}+S_{j,M}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{(E_{j,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

We similarly have that

(4.2)

R_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}-R_{j,M}^{\uparrow}=-B_{R,j,M}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}}+(T_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-B_{R,j,M}^{\uparrow}+S_{j,M}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{(E_{j,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

Step 2. Dominating the boundary length processes.
We are now going to define a sequence
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
which we can use to dominate
L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
from above and a sequence
\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
which we can use to dominate
R_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
from below.

We set
\widetilde{L}_{0,K}^{\uparrow}=0
. We define
\widetilde{L}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
using the recurrence relation for
j\geq 0

(4.3)

\widetilde{L}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{L}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}=(T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}-B_{L,j,K}^{\uparrow}-a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}){\bf 1}_{E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}}-B_{L,j,K}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

We then inductively define
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
for
M\leq K-1
by setting
\widetilde{L}_{0,M}^{\uparrow}=\widetilde{L}_{n_{M-1}^{\uparrow},M-1}^{\uparrow}
and we define
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
using the recurrence relation for
j\geq 0

(4.4)

\widetilde{L}_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}=(T_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-B_{L,j,M}^{\uparrow}-a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M}){\bf 1}_{E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}}-B_{L,j,M}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{(E_{j,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

Let
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}=\sum_{i=1}^{j}{\bf 1}_{(E_{i,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}}
and let
\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
be the sum of
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}(a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M})
and the
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
largest upward jumps made by either the left or the right boundary length processes up to the time that
\mathcal{N}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
has been explored. One can see that
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\leq L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
for all
j,M
because in the definition of the exploration, we have a slide to the left of size at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M}
on the top of each good chunk while in the definition of
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
we always have such a shift. Moreover, in the definition of
L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
we slide to the left over
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
loops of
\Gamma
previous discovered by the exploration as well as at most
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}(a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M})
units of extra length while
\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
gives the sum of the lengths of the longest
N_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
loops discovered by the exploration together with this extra length.

We also set
\widetilde{R}_{0,K}^{\uparrow}=0
. We define
\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
using the recurrence relation for
j\geq 0

(4.5)

\widetilde{R}_{j+1,K}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}=(a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}-B_{R,j,K}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}}+(T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}-B_{R,j,K}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

We then inductively define
\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
for
M\leq K-1
by setting
\widetilde{R}_{0,M}^{\uparrow}=\widetilde{R}_{n_{M-1}^{\uparrow},M-1}^{\uparrow}
and we define
\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
using the recurrence relation for
j\geq 0

(4.6)

\widetilde{R}_{j+1,M}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}=(a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)M}-B_{R,j,M}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{E_{j,M}^{\uparrow}}+(T_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-B_{R,j,M}^{\uparrow}){\bf 1}_{(E_{j,M}^{\uparrow})^{c}}.

One can similarly see that
\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\geq R_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
for all
j,M
.

Step 3. Analysis of dominating boundary length processes.

Lemma
C.2
implies that
\mathbf{E}[B_{R,j,K}^{\uparrow}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]\gtrsim(\log\delta_{0}^{-1})\delta_{0}^{\kappa/4}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
. We also have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}[T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}=2^{-K}\}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]+\mathbf{E}[T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}}{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}<2^{-K}\}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}].

Lemma
C.4
implies that the first term on the right hand side is at most a constant times
\delta_{0}^{\kappa/4}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
. For the second term on the right hand side, fix
p\in(1,4/\kappa)
and let
q>1
be such that
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[T_{j,K}^{\uparrow}{\bf 1}_{(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}}{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}<2^{-K}\}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}[(T_{jK}^{\uparrow})^{p}{\bf 1}_{\{\sigma_{j,K}^{\uparrow}<2^{-K}\}}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]^{1/p}\mathbf{P}[(E_{j,K}^{\uparrow})^{c}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]^{1/q}.

The first factor on the right hand side is at most a constant times
2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
by
[
4
, Chapter VIII, Proposition 4]
. The second term can be made arbitrarily small by making
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small. In particular, we choose
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small so that the second term is at most
\delta_{0}^{\kappa/4}
.

Therefore by choosing
\delta_{0}>0
sufficiently small and then
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small given our choice of
\delta_{0}
we see for a constant
d_{0}>0
that

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}[\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{R}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]\leq-d_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}.

As the increments
\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{R}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}-\mathbf{E}[\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{R}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{j-1,K}^{\uparrow}]
form a martingale difference sequence with a bounded
p
th moment for every
p\in(1,4/\kappa)
, it follows from (
4.7
) and
[
6
]
that for every
p\in(1,4/\kappa)
there exist constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0
so that

(4.8)

\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{R}_{N,K}^{\uparrow}\notin[-c_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}N,-c_{1}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}N]]\leq c_{2}N^{1-p}

and by Doob’s maximal inequality the same holds
\min_{1\leq n\leq N}\widetilde{R}_{n,K}^{\uparrow}
in place of
\widetilde{R}_{N,K}^{\uparrow}
. A similar analysis implies that there exist constants
c_{3},c_{4},c_{5}>0
so that

(4.9)

\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{L}_{N,K}^{\uparrow}\notin[c_{3}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}N,c_{4}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}N]]\leq c_{5}N^{1-p}

and by Doob’s maximal inequality the same holds
\max_{1\leq n\leq N}\widetilde{L}_{n,K}^{\uparrow}
in place of
\widetilde{L}_{n,K}^{\uparrow}
.

Let us now collect a few observations from the above. Let
\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow}
be the first
j
so that
\widetilde{R}_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
is at most
-2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
.

(i)

By (
4.8
), we have that

\mathbf{P}[c_{0}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}\leq\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}]=1-O(2^{-cK}).

(ii)

By (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we have that

\mathbf{P}[\widetilde{L}_{\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow},K}^{\uparrow}\geq c_{3}2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}]=1-O(2^{-cK}).

(iii)

On
\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}
, the amount of quantum natural time elapsed by the first
\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow}
chunks is at most
c_{1}2^{((1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)-1)K}
. Therefore (from the explicit form of the Lévy measure) on
\widetilde{n}_{K}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}
, the number of jumps that either the left or right boundary length processes make of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
is stochastically dominated by a Poisson random variable with mean a constant times

(2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K})^{-4/\kappa}\times 2^{((1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)-1)K}=2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4-1)K}.

As
\kappa\in(8/3,4)
we note that
(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4-1)<0
. Altogether, we conclude that the probability that either the left or right boundary length processes make a jump of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
is
O(2^{-cK})
.

Step 4. Analysis of boundary length processes.
Since we have that
\widetilde{L}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}-\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\leq L_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
and
\widetilde{R}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{j,M}^{\uparrow}\geq R_{j,M}^{\uparrow}
, it follows from the above and Lemma
C.5
that (possibly changing the values of
c,c_{0},c_{1}
) we have

(i)

\mathbf{P}[c_{0}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}\leq n_{K}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}]=1-O(2^{-cK}).

(ii)

We have that

\mathbf{P}[L_{n_{K}^{\uparrow},K}^{\uparrow}\geq c_{3}2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}]=1-O(2^{-cK}).

(iii)

The probability that either the left or right boundary length processes make a jump of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
is
O(2^{-cK})
.

Step 5. Completion of the proof.
On the event that the above hold, we consider the exploration starting at the next scale. As in the case
M=K
, we consider the dominating boundary length processes. We note that when we start the exploration for
M=K-1
, on the event described in (
ii
), the amount of boundary length to the left of the starting point which is contained in chunks for which
E_{j,K}^{\uparrow}
occurs is at least a constant times
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}
. On the event described in (
i
), the number of such chunks is at most
c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}
. Since the top boundary length of each such chunk is at most
C_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
, if
A
is the number of chunks with top boundary length at most
a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}
and
B
is the number of the remaining chunks, we have that

c_{3}2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)K}\leq a_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}\times A+C_{0}2^{-(\kappa/4)K}\times B.

By decreasing the value of
a_{0}>0
if necessary, the only way that this inequality can hold is if
B
is at least a constant times
2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)K}
.

The same analysis as for
M=K
thus implies for
M=K-1
that

(i)

By (
4.8
), we have that

\mathbf{P}[c_{0}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}\leq n_{M}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}]=1-O(2^{-cK}).

(ii)

By (
4.8
) and (
4.9
), we have that

\mathbf{P}[L_{n_{M}^{\uparrow},M}^{\uparrow}\geq c_{3}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}]=1-O(2^{-cM}).

(iii)

On
n_{M}^{\uparrow}\leq c_{1}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}
, the amount of quantum natural time elapsed by the chunks is at most
c_{1}2^{((1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)-1)M}
. Therefore (from the explicit form of the Lévy measure) the probability that either the left or right boundary length processes make a jump of size at least a constant times
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)M}
is
O(2^{-cM})
.

We can therefore iterate this for the rest of the steps in the upward exploration and likewise for the downward exploration to obtain the result.
∎

4.3.
Limiting exploration

The exploration described in Section
4.1
and the result of Lemma
4.1
from Section
4.2
shows that with probability
1-O(e^{-cJ})
there exists a path of “good chunks” in a percolation exploration which starts with a chunk corresponding to an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
run for time of order
2^{-K}
which forms a neighborhood of
0
and terminates precisely at
x_{1}
, the point on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
so that
\nu_{h}([0,x_{1}])=1
. The purpose of this section is to show that one can construct such an exploration which starts precisely at
0
and ends precisely at
x_{1}
by taking a limit as
K\to\infty
(Lemma
4.2
). We will then extend the construction to the setting of a quantum disk in Lemma
4.4
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{H}=(\mathbf{H},h,0,\infty)
is a quantum half-plane. Let
x_{1}\in\mathbf{R}_{+}
be such that
\nu_{h}([0,x_{1}])=1
. Fix
J\in\mathbf{N}
and suppose that we have families of events
E_{i,M}^{\uparrow}
,
E_{i,M}^{\downarrow}
for path-decorated quantum surfaces for each
i\in\mathbf{N}
and
M\geq J
so that if we fix
K\geq J
and perform the exploration as in Section
4.1
with
J\leq M\leq K
then the conditions of Lemma
4.1
are satisfied.

There exist families of quantum surfaces
(\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow})_{M\geq J}
and
(\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow})_{M\geq J}
coupled with
\mathcal{H}
so that the following are true.

(i)

\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}
is decreasing in
M
and
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}
is increasing in
M
.

(ii)

For each
M\geq J
, the conditional law of
\mathcal{H}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}
given
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}
is a quantum half-plane. The same holds with
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}
in place of
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}
.

(iii)

Fix
K\geq J
. The conditional law of the surfaces
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{K,J}^{\uparrow}
for
J\leq M\leq K
and
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{K,J}^{\uparrow}
for
J\leq M
given
\mathcal{E}_{K,J}^{\uparrow}
is described by the exploration procedure from Section
4.1
where we abuse notation and write
E_{i,M}^{\uparrow}
for the event that
E_{i,M}^{\uparrow}
holds for the path-decorated quantum surface up until when the
i
th
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
chunk is explored in
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}
. (In particular, each
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M+1,J}^{\uparrow}
and
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M-1,J}^{\downarrow}
can be further decomposed into a collection of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
chunks.)

Moreover, there exists a constant
c>0
(which does not depend on
J
) so that the probability that any of the following occur is
O(2^{-cJ})
:

•

There exists
M\geq J
so that the number of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
chunks which make up
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M+1,J}^{\uparrow}
for
J\leq M
or
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M-1,J}^{\downarrow}
for
J\leq M+2
is at least
c_{F}2^{(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}})(\kappa/4)M}
. The number of
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
chunks which make up
\mathcal{E}_{J,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{J,J}^{\uparrow}
is at least
c_{F}2^{(\kappa/4)J}
.

•

There exists
M\geq J
so that the boundary length process for an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
chunk which is part of either
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M+1,J}^{\uparrow}
or
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M-1,J}^{\downarrow}
makes a jump of size at least
2^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{PERC}}(\kappa/4)M}
.

•

Either
0
or
x_{1}
is disconnected from
\infty
by
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M+1,J}^{\uparrow}
or
\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\mathcal{E}_{M-1,J}^{\downarrow}
.

•

The difference in the boundary length of
\partial\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\setminus\partial\mathbf{H}
and
\partial\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow}\cap\partial\mathbf{H}
is at least
2^{-cM}
. The difference in the boundary length of
\partial\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\setminus\partial\mathbf{H}
and
\partial\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow}\cap\partial\mathbf{H}
is at least
2^{-cJ}
.

Moreover, the probability that any of the above three items occur for some fixed value of
M\geq J
is
O(2^{-cM})
(for the same constant
c
).

Before we proceed to the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we first need to collect the following lemma which will be used in order to show that the law of the diameter of the approximations to the
(\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\uparrow})_{M\geq J}
and
(\mathcal{E}_{M,J}^{\downarrow})_{M\geq J}
is tight. The proof of this lemma can be skipped on a first reading.

Figure 4.3.
Illustration of the setup and proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose that
-\infty<a_{1}<b_{1}<a_{2}<0<b_{2}<\infty
. For every
p\in(0,1)
there exists
d_{0}\in(0,\infty)
so that the following is true. Let
A
be a compact
\mathbf{H}
-hull (i.e.,
A\subseteq\mathbf{H}
,
A=\mathrm{cl}(A)\cap\mathbf{H}
,
\mathrm{cl}(A)
is compact, and
\mathbf{H}\setminus A
is simply connected) so that
\mathrm{cl}(A)\cap\partial\mathbf{H}\subseteq[a_{2},b_{2}]
. Let
\eta
be an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathbf{H}\setminus A
from
x\in[a_{1},b_{1}]
to
\infty
. If the probability that
\eta
hits
A
is at most
p
then
\mathrm{diam}(A)\leq d_{0}
.

Proof.

See Figure
4.3
for an illustration of the setup. Let
\psi
be the unique conformal transformation from
\mathbf{H}\setminus A
to
\mathbf{H}
which fixes
\infty
, takes
a_{1}
to
0
, and
b_{1}
to
1
. Then
\psi(\eta)
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa}^{0}(\kappa-6)
process in
\mathbf{H}
from
\psi(x)\in[0,1]
to
\infty
. Let
I=\psi(\mathrm{cl}(\mathbf{H}\cap\partial A))=[u,v]
. By assumption, the probability that
\psi(\eta)
hits
I
is at most
p
.

We claim that there exists
M\in(0,\infty)
depending only on
a_{1}
,
b_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
b_{2}
so that
u\leq M
. Indeed, suppose that
\eta^{\prime}
is an
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
b_{1}
to
\infty
. Then there exists
q\in(0,1)
so that the probability that
\eta^{\prime}
hits
[b_{2},\infty)
before hitting
(-\infty,a_{1}]
is at least
q
. Therefore the probability that
\eta^{\prime}
hits
A
or
[b_{2},\infty)
before hitting
(-\infty,a_{1}]
is also at least
q
. Now assume that
\eta^{\prime}
is an
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbf{H}\setminus A
instead of
\mathbf{H}
. By the locality property for
{\rm SLE}_{6}
, the same statement holds. Therefore the probability that
\psi(\eta^{\prime})
, an
{\rm SLE}_{6}
in
\mathbf{H}
from
1
to
\infty
, hits
\psi(\mathbf{H}\cap\partial A)\cup\psi([b_{2},\infty))\subseteq[u,\infty)
before hitting
(-\infty,0]
is at least
q
. This proves the claim.

Let
N\geq M
be such that the probability that
\psi(\eta)
hits
[M,N]
is at least
p
. We note that such an
N
exists as the trunk of
\eta
is an
{\rm SLE}_{\kappa^{\prime}}(\kappa^{\prime}/2-3;\kappa^{\prime}/2-3)
process in
\mathbf{H}
from
x
to
\infty
and the probability that such a process hits any infinite boundary interval is
1
. Note that
N
depends only on
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
b_{1}
,
b_{2}
, and
p
. Since the probability that
\psi(\eta)
hits
I
is at most
p
and
u\leq M
it must be that
v\leq N
.

To complete the proof, it suffices to show that for each
R\in(0,\infty)
there exists
d\in(0,\infty)
so that if
\mathrm{diam}(A)\geq d
then
v\geq R
. To see that this is the case, let
\eta^{\prime}
be as above. Suppose that we have
k\in\mathbf{N}
so that
A
crosses the annulus
A_{k}=B(0,2^{k})\setminus B(0,2^{k-1})
. Let
\tau_{k}=\inf\{t\geq 0:\eta^{\prime}(t)\in\partial B(0,3\cdot 2^{k-2})\}
(i.e.,
\tau_{k}
is the first time that
\eta^{\prime}
hits the circle in the middle of
A_{k}
) and let
\sigma_{k}=\inf\{t\geq\tau_{k}:\eta(t)\notin A_{k}\}
. If
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\tau_{k},\sigma_{k}]}
hits
[-2^{k},-2^{k-1}]
and then hits
[2^{k-1},2^{k}]
, then
\eta^{\prime}|_{[\tau_{k},\sigma_{k}]}
hits
A
. As there exists
p_{0}>0
so that the conditional probability of this event given
\eta^{\prime}|_{[0,\sigma_{k-1}]}
is at least
p_{0}
, it follows that if
